LSPU-LB ACADEMIC PROGRAMS: OVERVIEW
Laguna State Polytechnic University - Los Banos Campus (LSPU-LB) offers 23 degree programs across 8 colleges. All programs are four-year programs.

Colleges and the programs they offer:
- College of Arts and Sciences (CAS): Bachelor of Science in Psychology
- College of Business Administration and Accountancy (CBAA): Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Marketing Management; Bachelor of Science in Accountancy; Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Financial Management
- College of Computer Studies (CCS): Bachelor of Science in Computer Science - Major in Intelligent Systems; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Animation and Motion Graphics; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Web and Mobile Application Development; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Service Management Program
- College of Criminal Justice Education (CCJE): Bachelor of Science in Criminology
- College of Fisheries (CF): Bachelor of Science in Fisheries
- College of Food Nutrition and Dietetics (CFND): Bachelor of Science in Food Technology; Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics
- College of Hospitality Management and Tourism (CIHT): Bachelor of Science in Tourism Management; Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management
- College of Teacher Education (CTE): Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education - Major in Home Economics; Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education - Major in Food and Service Management; Bachelor of Physical Education; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in English; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Filipino; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Mathematics; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Sciences; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Social Studies; Bachelor of Elementary Education

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Computer Science - Major in Intelligent Systems
College: College of Computer Studies (CCS)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Computer Science (BSCS)
Major: Intelligent Systems
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers theoretical/mathematical computer science foundations (algorithms, computation theory, systems) and a standard software-engineering core (programming, databases, networks, security), with an artificial-intelligence specialization (machine learning, natural language processing, computer vision, robotics).

Skills developed:
- Programming Fundamentals
- Data Structures and Algorithm Design
- Database Design and Management
- Software Engineering Practice
- Networking and Information Security
- Machine Learning
- Natural Language Processing
- Computer Vision
- Robotics

Career and job outcomes:
- Machine Learning Engineer: Builds and deploys machine learning models for industry applications.
- Software Developer/Engineer: Designs, builds, and maintains software applications and systems.
- AI/Research Assistant: Supports research and development in computer vision, NLP, or robotics, or pursues graduate study in AI.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0
- Note: CCS gives priority to STEM and ICT/ICTS strand applicants.

Board/licensure status:
- Not a board program (no PRC licensure examination is required for this degree).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 25, s. 2015 - Revised Policies, Standards, and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Computer Science (BSCS), Bachelor of Science in Information Systems (BSIS), and Bachelor of Science in Information Technology (BSIT) Programs
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Design, develop, and evaluate computing solutions using appropriate current techniques, skills, and tools to meet the requirements of a specific computing or IT problem.
- Function effectively as part of a development team, communicating technical work clearly to both technical and non-technical stakeholders.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Marketing Management
College: College of Business Administration and Accountancy (CBAA)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Business Administration (BSBA)
Major: Marketing Management
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Trains students in core business principles (economics, management, business law, taxation, strategy) alongside specialized marketing coursework (consumer behavior, sales, distribution, advertising, product/pricing strategy, market research).

Skills developed:
- Marketing Strategy
- Selling and Negotiation
- Market Research
- Advertising and Campaign Development

Career and job outcomes:
- Marketing Associate/Officer: Works in brand management, digital marketing, or product marketing roles in private companies.
- Sales Executive/Account Manager: Manages client accounts and sales targets for a company's products or services.
- Market Research Analyst: Conducts consumer and market research to guide business and marketing decisions.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 88.0

Board/licensure status:
- Not a board program (no PRC licensure examination is required for this degree).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 17, s. 2017 - Revised Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Business Administration
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply core business, economic, and management principles to plan, organize, and evaluate business operations.
- Exercise sound, ethical decision-making within a chosen business specialization in response to a changing market environment.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Psychology
College: College of Arts and Sciences (CAS)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Psychology (BS Psychology)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Trains students in the scientific study of behavior and mental processes (developmental, abnormal, social, physiological psychology) alongside research methods and psychological assessment, preparing graduates for further specialization in clinical, industrial/organizational, or counseling psychology.

Skills developed:
- Psychological Assessment
- Psychological Research Methods and Statistics
- Counseling and Helping Relationship Skills
- Clinical Diagnosis and Case Formulation

Career and job outcomes:
- Clinical Psychology Track (Psychometrician / further studies toward licensure): Works as a psychometrician or pursues graduate study and supervised practice toward psychologist licensure under Philippine psychology practice law.
- Human Resources / Industrial-Organizational Psychology Track: Works in human resources, recruitment, training and organizational development roles that draw on the study of workplace behavior.
- Counseling / Guidance Track: Works in school or community guidance and counseling support roles, often alongside licensed guidance counselors.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 88.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Psychometrician Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 34, s. 2017 - Policies and Standards for Undergraduate Programs in Psychology
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply the scientific study of behavior and mental processes to understand individuals and groups across diverse settings.
- Conduct psychological research and assessment ethically, and pursue further training or licensure (e.g. as a psychometrician) as warranted.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Accountancy
College: College of Business Administration and Accountancy (CBAA)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Accountancy (BSA)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers financial and management accounting, auditing, taxation, and business law, preparing graduates for the CPA Licensure Examination.

Skills developed:
- Financial Accounting and Reporting
- Auditing
- Taxation
- Management Accounting and Cost Accounting

Career and job outcomes:
- Certified Public Accountant (CPA): Sits for the CPA Licensure Examination and practices in public accounting, private industry, government, or academia.
- Internal Auditor / Financial Analyst: Works in internal audit, financial reporting, or compliance functions within a company or government agency.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the CPA (Certified Public Accountant) Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 27, s. 2017 - Revised Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Accountancy (BSA)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply generally accepted accounting, auditing, and taxation principles to prepare and evaluate financial information with integrity.
- Qualify for and succeed in the CPA Licensure Examination and subsequent professional accountancy practice.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Criminology
College: College of Criminal Justice Education (CCJE)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Criminology (BSCrim)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers criminal law and procedure, criminalistics/forensic science, law enforcement administration, and correctional administration, preparing graduates for the Criminologist Licensure Examination.

Skills developed:
- Criminal Law and Procedure
- Forensic Science Fundamentals
- Crime Scene Investigation
- Law Enforcement Administration and Operations

Career and job outcomes:
- Criminologist / Law Enforcement Officer: Sits for the Criminologist Licensure Examination and pursues careers in the Philippine National Police, the Bureau of Jail Management and Penology, or related law enforcement and corrections agencies.
- Forensic/Crime Scene Investigator: Works in crime scene processing and forensic support roles for law enforcement agencies.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 85.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Criminologist Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 05, s. 2018 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Criminology (BS Criminology) Program
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply the principles of criminal law, criminalistics, and law enforcement administration to the investigation and prevention of crime.
- Practice the profession within Philippine legal and ethical standards for law enforcement, corrections, and criminal justice work.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Fisheries
College: College of Fisheries (CF)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Fisheries (BSF)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers aquaculture, fisheries biology and resource management, and fish post-harvest handling and processing.

Skills developed:
- Aquaculture Management
- Fish Stock and Fisheries Resource Assessment
- Fisheries Post-Harvest Handling and Processing

Career and job outcomes:
- Aquaculture Technician/Fish Farm Manager: Manages fish farming and aquaculture operations for private hatcheries, fish farms, or agribusiness companies.
- Fisheries Officer/Extension Worker: Works with government agencies such as the Bureau of Fisheries and Aquatic Resources on fisheries management and extension programs.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 87.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Fisheries Professional Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 33, s. 2021 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Fisheries
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply aquaculture, fisheries biology, and resource-management principles to sustainable fisheries production.
- Support fisheries-sector livelihoods and policy through post-harvest handling, extension work, or further study.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Food Technology
College: College of Food Nutrition and Dietetics (CFND)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Food Technology (BSFT)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers food processing, preservation, and product development along with food safety and quality control, preparing graduates for the Food Technologist Licensure Examination.

Skills developed:
- Food Processing and Preservation
- Food Safety and Quality Control
- Food Product Development

Career and job outcomes:
- Food Technologist: Sits for the Food Technologist Licensure Examination and works in food product development, processing, or quality assurance for food manufacturers.
- Quality Assurance/Quality Control Specialist: Oversees food safety and quality standards compliance in a food processing facility.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Food Technologist Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 07, s. 2019 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Food Technology
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Apply food science and engineering principles to the processing, preservation, and quality control of food products.
- Develop new or improved food products responsive to food safety regulations and consumer/industry needs.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics
College: College of Food Nutrition and Dietetics (CFND)
Degree: Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics (BSND)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: Covers clinical nutrition and diet therapy, community/public health nutrition, and institutional food service management, preparing graduates for the Nutritionist-Dietitian Licensure Examination.

Skills developed:
- Clinical Nutrition and Diet Therapy
- Community and Public Health Nutrition
- Institutional Food Service Operations

Career and job outcomes:
- Registered Nutritionist-Dietitian (RND): Sits for the Nutritionist-Dietitian Licensure Examination and works in hospitals, clinics, or community health programs.
- Institutional Food Service Supervisor: Manages meal planning and food service operations in hospitals, schools, or corporate cafeterias.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Nutritionist-Dietitian Licensure Examination.

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 14, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics (BSND) Program
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Assess and manage the nutritional needs of individuals and communities using sound clinical and public-health nutrition principles.
- Plan and oversee food service systems that meet nutritional, safety, and regulatory standards in institutional settings.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. PROGRAM: Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education - Major in Home Economics
College: College of Teacher Education (CTE)
Degree: Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education (BTLE)
Major: Home Economics
Duration: 4 years

Overview: A teacher-education program that prepares Technology and Livelihood Education (TLE)/Home Economics teachers for Grades 4-8, combined with TVET trainer-assessor accreditation and livelihood/entrepreneurship training.

Skills developed:
- Home Management and Livelihood Instruction
- TVET Training and Assessment Delivery
- Entrepreneurship and Livelihood Program Management

Career and job outcomes:
- Technology and Livelihood Education (TLE) Teacher, Grades 4-8: Teaches TLE/Home Economics exploratory subjects in the K-12 basic education curriculum after passing the Licensure Examination for Teachers.
- TVET Trainer-Assessor / Livelihood Program Facilitator: Works as an accredited TESDA trainer-assessor or facilitates community livelihood training programs.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 87.0
- Note: CTE additionally requires no grade lower than 85 in English, Mathematics, and Science.

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 78, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education (BTLEd)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Teach technology and livelihood education / home economics subjects using competency-based, hands-on instructional approaches.
- Design and facilitate livelihood or entrepreneurship training suited to community needs, including TESDA-aligned skills training.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

- Culinary Arts and Food Costing

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 87.0
- Note: CTE additionally requires no grade lower than 85 in English, Mathematics, and Science.

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 79, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education (BTVTEd)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Deliver technical-vocational instruction and competency assessment aligned with TESDA training regulations in a chosen specialization.
- Combine trade-specific technical skill with teaching pedagogy to prepare learners for both employment and further TVET study.

- Physical Fitness Assessment and Programming

Career and job outcomes:
- Physical Education Teacher / Sports Coach: Teaches PE in basic education or higher education, or coaches school/community sports teams, after passing the Licensure Examination for Teachers.
- Fitness Instructor/Program Coordinator: Works in gyms, sports facilities, or wellness programs designing and leading fitness programs.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0
- Note: CTE additionally requires no grade lower than 85 in English, Mathematics, and Science.

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 80, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Physical Education (BPEd)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Teach physical education and apply exercise science principles to promote learners' physical fitness and motor skill development.
- Plan, officiate, or coach sports and physical activity programs appropriate to varied age groups and settings.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

PROGRAM: Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in English
College: College of Teacher Education (CTE)
Degree: Bachelor of Secondary Education (BSEd)
Major: English
Duration: 4 years

Overview: A teacher-education program specializing in English language and literature, preparing graduates to teach English in junior/senior high school.

Skills developed:
- Language and Literature Teaching
- Subject-Area Teaching Pedagogy
- Curriculum Planning and Classroom Instruction

Career and job outcomes:
- High School English Teacher: Teaches English language and literature in junior/senior high school after passing the Licensure Examination for Teachers.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0
- Note: CTE additionally requires no grade lower than 85 in English, Mathematics, and Science.

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 75, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Secondary Education (BSEd)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Demonstrate mastery of subject-area content and pedagogy sufficient to teach that subject effectively at the junior/senior high school level.
- Design and implement curricula and assessments responsive to the diverse needs of secondary learners.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. PROGRAM: Bachelor of Elementary Education
College: College of Teacher Education (CTE)
Degree: Bachelor of Elementary Education (BEEd)
Duration: 4 years

Overview: A teacher-education program covering multi-subject elementary teaching methods and child-development-based instruction, preparing graduates to teach across subjects at the elementary level.

Skills developed:
- Multi-Subject Elementary Teaching Methods
- Child Development-Based Instruction

Career and job outcomes:
- Elementary School Teacher: Teaches multiple subjects at the elementary level after passing the Licensure Examination for Teachers.

Admission requirement:
- Minimum General Weighted Average (GWA): 90.0
- Note: CTE additionally requires no grade lower than 85 in English, Mathematics, and Science.

Board/licensure status:
- Board program. Graduates may take the Licensure Examination for Teachers (Elementary Level).

Regulatory basis (CHED): CMO No. 74, s. 2017 - Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Elementary Education (BEEd)
Program educational objectives (per this CMO):
- Demonstrate deep and principled understanding of how elementary learners grow and learn across the full range of school subjects taught at that level.
- Facilitate learning using a wide range of teaching methodologies delivered in varied learning environments, in and outside the traditional classroom.

Source notes: admission cutoff from the LSPU Admission Guidelines, 1st Semester AY 2026-2027, Annex A. Overview, skills, career outcomes, objectives, and board status reflect what the program's CMO and general knowledge of the field typically cover; they are not an official LSPU curriculum document.

BOARD (LICENSURE) PROGRAMS VS NON-BOARD PROGRAMS AT LSPU-LB
Board programs (graduates may sit a PRC licensure examination):
- Bachelor of Science in Psychology: Psychometrician Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Science in Accountancy: CPA (Certified Public Accountant) Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Science in Criminology: Criminologist Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Science in Fisheries: Fisheries Professional Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Science in Food Technology: Food Technologist Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics: Nutritionist-Dietitian Licensure Examination
- Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education - Major in Home Economics: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education - Major in Food and Service Management: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Physical Education: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in English: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Filipino: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Mathematics: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Sciences: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Social Studies: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Secondary Level)
- Bachelor of Elementary Education: Licensure Examination for Teachers (Elementary Level)

Non-board programs (no PRC licensure examination):
- Bachelor of Science in Computer Science - Major in Intelligent Systems
- Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Marketing Management
- Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Financial Management
- Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Animation and Motion Graphics
- Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Web and Mobile Application Development
- Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Service Management Program
- Bachelor of Science in Tourism Management
- Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management

CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 25, s. 2015
Title: Revised Policies, Standards, and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Computer Science (BSCS), Bachelor of Science in Information Systems (BSIS), and Bachelor of Science in Information Technology (BSIT) Programs
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Computer Science - Major in Intelligent Systems; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Animation and Motion Graphics; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Web and Mobile Application Development; Bachelor of Science in Information Technology - Major in Service Management Program.
Program educational objectives:
- Design, develop, and evaluate computing solutions using appropriate current techniques, skills, and tools to meet the requirements of a specific computing or IT problem.
- Function effectively as part of a development team, communicating technical work clearly to both technical and non-technical stakeholders.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 74, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Elementary Education (BEEd)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Elementary Education.
Program educational objectives:
- Demonstrate deep and principled understanding of how elementary learners grow and learn across the full range of school subjects taught at that level.
- Facilitate learning using a wide range of teaching methodologies delivered in varied learning environments, in and outside the traditional classroom.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 75, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Secondary Education (BSEd)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in English; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Filipino; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Mathematics; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Sciences; Bachelor of Secondary Education - Major in Social Studies.
Program educational objectives:
- Demonstrate mastery of subject-area content and pedagogy sufficient to teach that subject effectively at the junior/senior high school level.
- Design and implement curricula and assessments responsive to the diverse needs of secondary learners.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 05, s. 2018
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Criminology (BS Criminology) Program
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Criminology.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply the principles of criminal law, criminalistics, and law enforcement administration to the investigation and prevention of crime.
- Practice the profession within Philippine legal and ethical standards for law enforcement, corrections, and criminal justice work.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 07, s. 2019
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Food Technology
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Food Technology.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply food science and engineering principles to the processing, preservation, and quality control of food products.
- Develop new or improved food products responsive to food safety regulations and consumer/industry needs.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 14, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics (BSND) Program
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Nutrition and Dietetics.
Program educational objectives:
- Assess and manage the nutritional needs of individuals and communities using sound clinical and public-health nutrition principles.
- Plan and oversee food service systems that meet nutritional, safety, and regulatory standards in institutional settings.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 17, s. 2017
Title: Revised Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Business Administration
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Marketing Management; Bachelor of Science in Business Administration - Major in Financial Management.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply core business, economic, and management principles to plan, organize, and evaluate business operations.
- Exercise sound, ethical decision-making within a chosen business specialization in response to a changing market environment.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 27, s. 2017
Title: Revised Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Accountancy (BSA)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Accountancy.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply generally accepted accounting, auditing, and taxation principles to prepare and evaluate financial information with integrity.
- Qualify for and succeed in the CPA Licensure Examination and subsequent professional accountancy practice.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 33, s. 2021
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for the Bachelor of Science in Fisheries
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Fisheries.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply aquaculture, fisheries biology, and resource-management principles to sustainable fisheries production.
- Support fisheries-sector livelihoods and policy through post-harvest handling, extension work, or further study.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 34, s. 2017
Title: Policies and Standards for Undergraduate Programs in Psychology
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Psychology.
Program educational objectives:
- Apply the scientific study of behavior and mental processes to understand individuals and groups across diverse settings.
- Conduct psychological research and assessment ethically, and pursue further training or licensure (e.g. as a psychometrician) as warranted.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 62, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Science in Tourism Management (BSTM) and Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management (BSHM)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Science in Tourism Management; Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management.
Program educational objectives:
- Plan, deliver, and manage guest-centered tourism or hospitality operations that meet industry service standards.
- Advance to supervisory or entrepreneurial roles within the tourism and hospitality industry through demonstrated operational and management competence.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 78, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education (BTLEd)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Technology and Livelihood Education - Major in Home Economics.
Program educational objectives:
- Teach technology and livelihood education / home economics subjects using competency-based, hands-on instructional approaches.
- Design and facilitate livelihood or entrepreneurship training suited to community needs, including TESDA-aligned skills training.
Verification note: Sourced from CMO No. 78, s. 2017; the number 78 is corroborated across multiple independent university BTLEd program pages, but cmoTitle's exact wording is reconstructed from the standard CHED PSG naming pattern rather than copied from the primary CMO PDF — verify before quoting it as the exact official title.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 79, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education (BTVTEd)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Technical-Vocational Teacher Education - Major in Food and Service Management.
Program educational objectives:
- Deliver technical-vocational instruction and competency assessment aligned with TESDA training regulations in a chosen specialization.
- Combine trade-specific technical skill with teaching pedagogy to prepare learners for both employment and further TVET study.
Verification note: Sourced from CMO No. 79, s. 2017; the number 79 is corroborated by an academic journal article and independent BTVTEd curriculum documents, but cmoTitle's exact wording is reconstructed from the standard CHED PSG naming pattern rather than copied from the primary CMO PDF — verify before quoting it as the exact official title.

======================================================================
CHED MEMORANDUM ORDER: CMO No. 80, s. 2017
Title: Policies, Standards and Guidelines for Bachelor of Physical Education (BPEd)
LSPU-LB programs covered by this CMO: Bachelor of Physical Education.
Program educational objectives:
- Teach physical education and apply exercise science principles to promote learners' physical fitness and motor skill development.
- Plan, officiate, or coach sports and physical activity programs appropriate to varied age groups and settings.
Verification note: Sourced from CMO No. 80, s. 2017; corroborated by one university department page plus CHED's own confirmation that CMOs 74-80 and 82, s. 2017 form one teacher-education family (74=BEEd and 75=BSEd match the user-supplied list), but this is the weakest-sourced of the three added CMOs — only one direct source ties the number specifically to BPEd, and cmoTitle's wording is reconstructed, not copied from the primary CMO PDF. Verify before treating as final.

I. | Mandate:

The University shall primarily provide advanced education, professional, technological,
and vocational instruction in agriculture, fisheries, forestry, science, engineering,
industrial technologies, teacher education, medicine, law, arts and sciences,
information technology, and other fields. It shall also undertake research and extension
services, and provide progressive leadership in its areas of specialization

II. | Vision:

An ASEAN Polytechnic University by 2030.

III. | Mission:

A premier ASEAN polytechnic university that LEADS in multidisciplinary education and
applied research, equipping future-ready servant LEADers to drive innovation for
sustainable regional development.

IV. | Service Pledge:
We, the members of the Faculty and Staff of the University, do pledge and commit
to:

Lead in providing quality education extension services and transfer of technology
services to the communities;

Serve motherland and humanity at all times;

Perform with utmost fairness, honesty and accountability;
Undertake our responsibilities in the pursuit of common goals and welfare.

computer systems. By conducting regular inspections, cleanings, and adjustments,
potential issues can be identified and resolved before they escalate into costly
problems. This service helps the university keep its computers running smoothly,
minimizing downtime and maximizing productivity.

configurations, network settings, and user profiles. It ensures accurate

# PILLAR 2  |  PART B: LSPU ADMISSION GUIDELINES, 1ST SEM AY 2026-2027 (LSPU-LB)
################################################################################

Republic of the Philippines
Laguna State Polytechnic University
Province of Laguna
Office of the Guidance and Counseling

LSPU ADMISSION GUIDELINES
FOR THE FIRST (1ST) SEMESTER OF AY 2026-2027

Instruction: Please read carefully the following guidelines to pre-evaluate the qualifications of the applicant following the admission requirements of the university and colleges.

For information and guidance, the admission guidelines are presented below for college applicants (incoming first-year and transfer students) for the First Semester of the Academic Year 2026-2027 at the LSPU-Los Baños Campus of the Laguna State Polytechnic University (anticipated start of classes: August 2026).

These guidelines cover the following items:
B. Admission Requirements................................................................Page 2
C. Admission Process.........................................................................Page 2
D. Contact Information........................................................................Page 3
E. Annex A: List of Curricular Program Offerings and
   General Weighted Average Requirement.........................................Page 4


B. ADMISSION REQUIREMENTS

The requirements listed below should be in PDF format for uploading in the LSPU Online Application System. The clear and readable documents to be uploaded will be verified and serve as the basis of qualifications.

NOTE: No original copies of documents will be asked during the admission process. Please keep them for the enrolment process.

1. College Qualification
   General Weighted Average (GWA)
   As stipulated in the LSPU Handbook 2021, the colleges may set their respective qualifications on the general weighted average (GWA) of students to be admitted to the program (board or non-board) in consideration of accommodation capacity and standards. For the list of programs and their respective GWA requirements, please see "Annex A" on page 4.

2. Documentary Requirements

   For First Year College (Freshmen) Applicants:
   a. Recent 2x2 ID Picture (white background)
   b. Birth Certificate (PSA/NSO)
   c. Certificate of Good Moral Character (with certification of current grade level and school year)
   d. Form 138 (authenticated) or Certified True Copy of SF9 (first semester Grade 12 Grades/Report Card signed by Adviser/Principal)

   For Transfer Student Applicants:
   a. Recent 2x2 ID Picture (white background)
   b. Birth Certificate (PSA/NSO)
   c. Certificate of Good Moral Character (with certification of current year level and school year)
   d. Certificate True Copy of Grades


C. ADMISSION PROCESS

Step 1: Online Application.
Accomplish the LSPU Online Application (portal: https://admissions.lspusys.com/). Make sure to input true and correct information on the required fields properly.

Step 2: Examination Schedule.
Check email for the schedule of examination to be sent by the Admission/Guidance Office. Note: Make sure that the email address to be used in the application is correct and active.

Step 3: Admission Examination.
Take the scheduled Admission Examination at the designated Testing Room of the campus as indicated in the email. Bring the authenticated Form 138 or Certified True Copy of SF9 (first semester Grade 12 Grades/Report Card signed by Adviser/Principal) for verification, as well as the application form printed in A4 paper. Note: A college examination may also be implemented according to the college's standards.

Step 4: Interview Schedule.
Check email for the schedule of College Interview to be sent by the College.

Step 5: Interview.
Attend the scheduled interview with the College Dean or program coordinator/designated faculty.

Step 6: Posting of Results.
Check the posting of qualified applicants by the Admission/Guidance Office. Check the official Facebook page of LSPU for updates and announcements (http://facebook.com/LSPUOfficial).

Step 7: Medical Examination.
Accomplish the medical examination to be submitted to the University Clinic. Requirements include the following: medical certificate from accredited clinical laboratory, drug test, and x-ray. Note: Processing of medical requirements is highly encouraged to be done with the University-accredited medical facilities; however, other medical facilities are also accepted.

Step 8: Enrolment.
Proceed with the enrolment on the respective schedule. A separate announcement will be posted for the enrolment procedure.


D. CONTACT INFORMATION

For concerns or inquiries regarding the admission process, please refer to the following contact information of the Guidance and Counseling offices (Admission Office) below:

- Guidance and Counseling (LSPU Los Baños Campus)
  Email Address: guidance_lb@lspu.edu.ph
  Facebook Page: Guidance Office-LSPU Los Baños Campus

FOREWORD Every educational institution has its special mission and unique ways to bring about the total development of the persons who are entrusted to it and who pledged to abide by its policies. LSPU system has been founded to help each student to be developed into a total person who is well equipped with knowledge and skill, fits the modern world of technologies, and is ready and capable of meeting the challenges of life.

This student handbook has been prepared to help students become positively aware of their academic and social tasks. The policies and regulations contained in this handbook seek to establish and ensure an atmosphere of qualified freedom, justice, and love in the academic community that is necessary to carry out the institution's missions and objectives.

The contents of this handbook cover all undergraduate and graduate students currently enrolled in this State University who, upon admission, have agreed to abide and conduct themselves accordingly to maintain self-discipline and uphold the LSPU system's policies, rules and regulations—hence, promoting the institution's standard of excellence.

It is hoped that with the full knowledge of the policies, the whole studentry of all campuses will generously and willingly cooperate with the administration in achieving the success of the system's educational endeavor.

MESSAGE OF THE UNIVERSITY PRESIDENT To All Students, With this student handbook, you will learn how Laguna State Polytechnic University (LSPU) operates and thereby how you, as a student, can be in harmony with the institution. This handbook is designed to give you the necessary information to succeed in your academic life and develop yourself into productive individuals. It offers a host of practical information that will guide you in getting things done accordingly. It reminds you to be observant and aware of what you should or should not do as students of the University. With the help of its guiding policies, may you bring with you a homeschool atmosphere and self-fulfillment within the campus's shelter.

This handbook tells you where to go and what is offered in the University. Do not hesitate to keep in touch and consult with the Director/ Chairperson for Student Affairs and Services, the Deans/Associate Deans, and the other university officials responsible for your welfare and whose roles are laid out here. Use this handbook as a guide in finding the right person to help you with any university-related concerns. We remain our relationship with students transparent.

Provided with this guide, we wish our students to live by the LSPU core values. The University prides itself on being friendly, just as it is intellectually ambitious and innovative at the same time. LSPU works as a community because of the personal decency and mutual respect amongst administrators, faculty, personnel, and students. We respect all who come here. Likewise, we expect you to help promote the LSPU culture of honesty, courtesy, and politeness.

As it is designed, I hope this handbook serves as a useful, clear, and comprehensive guide for you to enjoy your stay here at the university. Together, let us join hands in transforming your experiences into meaningful strengths.

Welcome to the LSPU family!

Mario R. Briones, Ed.D.

University President VISION The Laguna State Polytechnic University is a center of technological innovation that promotes interdisciplinary learning, sustainable utilization of resources, collaboration and partnership with the community and stakeholders.

MISSION LSPU, driven by progressive leadership, is a premier institution providing technology-mediated agriculture, fisheries, and other related and emerging disciplines significantly contributing to the growth and development of the region and nation.

LSPU QUALITY POLICY LSPU delivers quality education through responsive instruction, distinctive research, sustainable extension, and production services. Thus, we commit to continually improve to meet applicable requirements to provide quality, efficient and effective services to the university stakeholders’ highest level of satisfaction through an excellent management system imbued with utmost integrity, professionalism, and innovation.

(BOR Resolution 095, s.2021)

PRAYER Almighty Creator, grant me a teachable spirit that I may learn from the students, teachers, and staff I will encounter in this university.

Let every experience in my stay here be an opportunity for me to grow academically, socially, and emotionally.

Help me see the beauty of perseverance that no matter how hard a situation I may face, I will look at it as a challenge I shall overcome on my journey into the realization of my dreams.

In You I trust, Always.

Chapter 1 LSPU HISTORICAL DEVELOPMENT AND OFFICIALS LSPU Historical Development The Laguna State Polytechnic University (LSPU) was initially established in 1952 as a provincial secondary school known as Baybay Provincial High School, the first public high school in the shoreline district of Laguna de Bay. In 1957, it was converted into Baybay Agricultural and Vocational School

(BNAVS) by Republic Act (RA) No.1807.

With the passage of House Bill (HB) 269 into law in June 1971, Rep. Act No. 6327, BNAVS was then converted into Baybay National College of Agriculture and Technology (BNCAT), followed by its conversion into a state college known as Laguna State Polytechnic College (LSPC) on June 10, 1983.

Laguna State Polytechnic College grew more prominent and better from then on with the incumbency of Dr. Ricardo A. Wagan as the first College President in 1986. Through Memoranda of Agreements with the local government officials, Dr. Wagan realized the establishment of satellite campuses such as LSPC Nagcarlan Satellite Campus (S.Y. 1991-1992); LSPC Santa Maria Satellite Campus (S.Y. 1992-1993); and LSPC Cabuyao Campus (S.Y. 1993-1994). The integration phase of three (3) Commission on Higher Education (CHED) Supervised Institutions by the Special Provision No. 2 of the CHED FY 1999 Budget under the General Appropriations Act of 1999 transpired under Dr. Wagan’s incumbency.

In January 1999, the former Laguna College of Arts and Trades in Santa Cruz, Laguna, was integrated to LSPC by R.A. 8745, R.A. 7722, and R.A. 8292, hence known as Laguna State Polytechnic College-Santa Cruz Campus. In the same year, Los Baños College of Fisheries at Los Baños Laguna was also integrated to LSPC by R.A. 7722, known as the Higher Education Act of 1994, and R.A. 8292, Higher Education Modernization Act of 1997.

Under CHED Memorandum Order No. 27, s. 2000, Implementing Guidelines of the Integration of CHED-Supervised Institutions (CSIs) to State Universities and Colleges (SUCs) Phase II, the formal turn-over ceremony took place relative to the integration of San Pablo City National School of Arts and Trades at San Pablo City to LSPU.

President Wagan marked remarkable and distinctive achievements for LSPC. The established multi-campus institution amplified the number of nondegree and degree curricular programs/offerings that necessitated hiring more teachers due to increased enrollment. There was also intensified rehabilitation, repairing, and construction of academic buildings and relevant facilities. Graduate programs were launched, which paved the way for acquiring state-of-theart equipment.

Likewise, President Wagan opened doors to neighboring Asian countries through academic and cultural exchange and partnership programs. He signed numerous memoranda of agreements (MOAs) and sisterhood agreements with Changwon College and Changwon High School in South Korea and Thai Nguyen University of Agriculture and Forestry in Vietnam.

In the 13th Congress of the Philippines, during its 3rd Regular Session, R.A. No. 9402 converted Laguna State Polytechnic College into a State University, appropriating funds thereof; approved by the Philippine President's Office on March 22, 2007, with Dr. Ricardo A. Wagan as its first University President.

Today, LSPU offers relevant, affordable, and accredited fourteen nonduplicated curricula programs. Nurtured by Dr. Nestor M. De Vera, the 2nd university president, LSPU established an additional satellite campus in Lopez Quezon (S.Y. 2011-2012) with curricular programs in Agriculture and Fisheries and LSPU Extension Magdalena Manpower Training Center (S.Y. 2014-2015).

In addition to the existing international academic programs, the excellent and strong leadership of President De Vera had established partnerships with universities along the Asia Pacific Region such as Phnom Penh International University in Cambodia; Saigon Technology University, and Nong Lam University in Ho Chi Minh City and Hung Vuong University in Phu Tho Province, SRV; and Yunnan Agricultural University in China.

Supporting the Philippines ' National Development Agenda of Human Development and Poverty Reduction and Economic Development, LSPU helps the government make education the central strategy for investing in human resources to reduce poverty while inviting economic growth and national competitiveness. The university tries to achieve the center of sustainable development and higher-level accredited status, under the equally dynamic and innovative administration of its 2nd University President, Dr. Nestor M. De Vera.

LSPU is a SUC Level III Higher Education Institution, while most of the academic programs are accredited by the AACCUP ranging from Level III reaccredited status to preliminary status for new programs. The BSED, BSIE, and Industrial Technology programs are candidates for Level IV reaccreditation status, while the Teacher Education Program of San Pablo City campus is awarded the Center of Development (2016). Likewise, the university's four campuses received an ISO 9001: 2015 Certification in 2018 for its quality management system while passing the Institutional Accreditation, Level I for its quality institutional operation.

Dr. Eden C. Callo was designated as officer-in-charge, Office of the LSPU President, while the search for the 3rd university president was ongoing. (CHED Special Order 26, s.2019) On May 22, 2019, Dr. Mario R. Briones was named as the 3rd LSPU President. (BOR Resolution 1410, s.2019) The leadership of President Briones during the first year had shown the following accomplishments: approval of the enhanced curriculum (CMO PSG 2017), establishment of the LSPU-Liliw Pilot-extension classes in Liliw, Laguna (BOR Resolution 21, s.2019); nationally recognized as an ISO 9001:2015 government higher education institution, Regional PASUC Chairperson, reconstruction of the university’s organizational structure, and review and updating of all the university’s manuals of operations for quality management system.

The onslaught of the COVID 19 pandemic brought an unprecedented change in the educational landscape of the university. In order to cope with the new normal, President Briones initiated the conceptualization of the LSPU curriculum and instruction framework for the new normal. The LSPU Board of Regents approved the university’s concepts and processes in migrating to the new normal, adopting Flexible Learning as a Delivery Model (BOR Resolution 61, s.2020), and LSPU Pandemic Plan based on a series of institutional studies and workshops for implementation on September 21, 2020.

Chapter 3

UNDERGRADUATE ACADEMIC POLICIES

Article 1. Classifications of Students

Sec. 1. Based on Admission

1.1 New Student. A student who is enrolled in the university for the first time.

1.2. Transferee Student. A student who had partly finished his/her course in other schools and satisfied the college's weighted average. All subjects taken shall be credited if compared with the university subjects.

1.3. Cross-enrollee Student. A student currently enrolled in another campus/school is permitted to enroll in the university to earn credit units or vice versa.

1.4. Foreign Student. A student whose citizenship is not Filipino who enrolls in any degree program of the university.

1.5. Returnee Student. A student who is previously enrolled in the University and after having stopped without enrolling elsewhere or has valid leaves of absence for the period during which the student discontinued the studies and is not disqualified from continuing their university studies.

Sec. 2. Based on Curriculum Level

2.1 Freshman. A student who has earned a total or fewer than twentyfive percent (25%) of the entire units required in the whole course.

2.2 Sophomore. A student who has earned a total of twenty-five percent to fifty percent (25%-50%) of the total units required in the entire course.

2.3. Junior. A student who has earned fifty to seventy-five (50%-75%) or more of the total units required in the entire course.

2.4. Senior. A student who has earned seventy-five (75%) or more of the total units required in the entire course.

Sec. 3. Based on Placement

3.1. Full time/Regular. A student who carries the full load for a given term/semester/summer term prescribed in the curriculum in which he is enrolled each semester. Credits earned in some of the required subjects will be added to those taken to determine status.

3.2. Irregular. A student who carries less than the full load prescribed in the curriculum at a given time.

Article 2. Admission Requirements

Sec. 1. LSPU Entrance Examination Instrument, Policies and Guidelines for Admission, and Retention (CMO 105, s. 2015)

1.1. Accommodation Capacity. Admission to this University shall be limited to the number of students for whom adequate facilities and competent faculty members are available to provide better instruction.

1.2. Free Higher Education. All students who are either currently enrolled at the time of the affectivity of the Universal Access to Quality Tertiary Education Act of 2017 or Unified Financial Assistance System for Tertiary Education Act, or UniFAST (Republic Act No.

10687) shall enroll at any time after that in a course leading to a bachelor’s degree in the University shall be exempt from paying tuition and other school fees for units enrolled in.

1.3. Institutional Qualifications. Students are eligible to receive the benefits stipulated in 1.2. provided that they qualify the admission and retention policies of the University.

Sec. 2. Admission and Testing

2.1. Non-discriminatory Admission. No student shall be denied admission to the University by reason of age, sex, gender, nationality, belief, or political affiliation. Equal opportunity is also granted to persons with disabilities (PWDs), differently-abled, indigenous people, solo parents, and people with special concerns; they are likewise admitted for enrolment.

2.2. Foreign Students Admission. International students may be admitted to the College of their choice in accordance with the guidelines set by the Philippine Bureau of Immigration (BI), Commission on Higher Education (CHED), and the University. Provided further that Filipino applicants have priority over them, and finally, a reciprocal agreement exists between the foreigner’s country and the Philippines. Expressly, international student visas may be accepted for regular students upon compliance with the following requirements and policies:

2.2.1. Non-immigrant or foreign student-applicants who graduated from a high school abroad and have not enrolled in the university may be admitted to the freshmen class.

2.2.2. The foreign applicant must be a graduate of recognized secondary schools or colleges in their country of origin and submit complete documents. Transferees must show records or proofs of current registration or completion of term course.

2.2.3. Qualifying in the university national or international foreign administered examination such as the General Certificate of Education (GCE) Examination or the Scholastic Aptitude Test (SAT) or any equivalent examination.

2.2.3.1. GCE: 3 ordinary level passed and two advanced levels passed

2.2.3.2. Scholastic Aptitude Test: Minimum total score of 1200

2.2.3.3. IBE (International Baccalaureate Diploma)

2.2.4. The foreign student applicant who is not a native English speaker is required to have at least earned a minimum score of 500 in the Test of English as Foreign Language

(TOEFL) or completion of the Intensive English Language Course (IELP) at LSPU or another reputable English Language training institution to equip the students with their Basic Interpersonal Language Skills (BICS) and Cognitive Academic Language Proficiency (CALP).

2.2.5 Students who can present proof of adequate English proficiency, e.g., IELTS Certificate or related certificates, may not need to enroll in the IELP Course.

2.2.6 Filipinos who graduate from a secondary school abroad applying for freshman admission must satisfy the same requirements as those for international students.

2.2.7. Upon completing the IELP, foreign/international students are subjected to the university's admission policies and rules and regulations.

2.3. Credential Requirements. No applicant for enrolment, whether old or new student, is allowed without presentation of proper credentials.

2.4. Admission Examinations. Student applicants should pass the following admission requirements administered by the Guidance Office:

2.4.1. Admission Test. It aims to measure the ability of the students.

2.4.2 University Qualifying Examination. It aims to measure the readiness and the competencies of students for the university.

2.4.3. Results shall satisfy the required standards of the chosen College.

2.4.4. An interview is also a part of the admission.

2.5. Percentile Rank. Students who meet the required percentile rank are eligible to enroll in their chosen field of study and are subjected to the College's screening policies.

2.6. College/Department Qualification. As a policy, students should have the following General Weighted Average (GWA) in their Form 138 for the following priority courses unless otherwise specified by the College:

2.6.1. eighty-five (85) and above for Accountancy, Engineering, Nursing, Information Technology, Psychology, Biology, Mathematics, Criminology, Agriculture, Teacher Education, and other Board Programs

2.6.2. eighty-two (82) and above – Hotel Restaurant Management, Tourism, and Industrial Technology

2.6.3. For courses with board examination like teacher education, engineering, accountancy, nursing, criminology, among others, students shall not have a grade in their Form 138 below eighty-five (85), or otherwise specified by the College, at any of the following subjects: English, Science, and Mathematics.

2.7. Pledge of Compliance. The student and the parent/guardian should sign the pledge that the student will abide/comply with all the university's rules and regulations. Refusal to take this pledge or violation of its term shall be sufficient for summary dismissal or denial of admission. (Appendix E)

2.8. Certificate of Registration. No student should be admitted to classes unless the Certificate of Registration (COR) or a document certifying that the students are officially enrolled had been duly signed by the concerned authorities.

2.9. Transferring. Those who intend to transfer to another College should satisfy its grade requirements. The prescribed forms for transfer should indicate a valid reason with an attached copy of grades and a recommendation letter from the previous Dean with a courtesy copy furnished to the registrar. Approval of the College Dean and the Registrar should be acquired before twelve percent (12%) of the regular class meeting for the terms is held. Finally, the student must have completed in the State University no less than sixty percent (60%) of the program's units and seventy-five (75%) if running for honors. (Also see Residency).

2.10. Shifting of Course. Students from other courses can shift to any BS Program within the university provided that the following conditions are met:

2.10.1. No failure of greater than six (6) units during the semester.

2.10.2. No failure in the course/major-related subjects stated in the retention policy of any BS in the respective program curriculum.

2.10.3 A filled out shifting form from the Office of the Registrar

2.11. Basic Requirements. A student seeking admission to the University must comply with the following minimum requirements:

2.11.1. Freshmen - Report Card (DepEd Form 138) - Permanent Record (DepEd Form 137) - physical, medical, and drug testing examinations certified by a government physician, certifying the applicant’s fitness to do University work - Certificate of Good Moral Character (the last school attended) - three (3) latest 2x2 pictures - photocopy of authenticated birth certificate/marriage certificate (for married female students only) issued by the Philippine Statistics Authority (PSA), formerly National Statistics Office (NSO) - Alternative Learning System (ALS)/Philippine Educational Placement Test (PEPT) results (to graduates of any of these programs)

2.11.2. Student Transferees - Transfer Credential/Honorable Dismissal from the school last attended - Transcript of Records or a copy of Academic Records for evaluation purposes - physical, medical, and drug testing examinations certified by a government physician, certifying the applicant’s fitness to do University work - Certificate of Good Moral Character (the last school attended) - three (3) latest 2x2 pictures - photocopy of authenticated birth certificate/marriage certificate (for married female students only) issued by the Philippine Statistics Authority (PSA), formerly NSO

2.13. Other requirements/s may be added as deemed necessary to any of the programs offered by the College/Department. Likewise, payment for accomplishing requirements (i.e., medical test, drug test, etc.) should be shouldered by the student/applicant, especially for testing done in an outside facility.

2.14 Non-regular Admission

2.14.1 Non-degree Students. A degree holder or undergraduate student who is not currently enrolled in any other higher learning institution may be allowed to take the credit courses on the graduate and/or undergraduate level, respectively, provided that this student satisfies the appropriate requirements for admission to the college. He/she should not be allowed to enroll for more than one semester, except by special permission of the dean of the college concerned and the university registrar.

Since the applicant does not follow any organized study program, a non-degree student is not a prospective candidate for graduation for any degree in the university.

2.14.2. Special Student. Even if he does not fully satisfy the university entrance requirements, a mature student may be admitted as a special student and may enroll for any subject that is, in the dean's opinion, has the necessary information or ability to pursue profitably. He/she should not be allowed to enroll for more than nine (9) except by special permission from the

VPAA.

Subjects taken shall be noncredit, although his work may be reported at the end of each semester as satisfactory or unsatisfactory.

The special student may register at any time without the payment of fine for late registration subject to another college regulation.

2.15. Online Admission. Online admission makes it easy and convenient for prospective students to apply for various programs offered by the university during the admission cycle using internet connectivity.

Article 3. Registration. Registration is open to new and old students who have satisfied the admission requirements. For on-site registration, students should enroll at the Office of the Registrar and register only in those subjects approved by the Registration Adviser/Program Coordinator/College Dean, as evidenced by their initials on the registration form. Whenever applicable, online registration may be used in line with its effectivity as endorsed by the University Registrar.

Sec. 1. Late Registration. The students are still allowed to register one (1) week after the last day of the regular registration period. Late registrants are charged a fine of P500.00. (BOR Resolution 1046 s 2012)

Sec. 2. Deferment of Enrolment. In the event that a qualified freshman applicant, who, for a valid reason, cannot enroll during the semester initially applied for, may petition for deferment of enrolment to the succeeding semester by writing to the University Registrar, provided that the applicant would not take any academic subjects in another college/ university during the deferment period.

Sec. 3. Refunding of Fees. A student who has paid the enrolment fees and who is granted Honorable Dismissal/Transfer Credentials or Leave of Absence (LOA) is entitled to a refund of their fees, except matriculation or registration fee, in accordance with the following schedule:

3.1. seventy-five percent (75%): within the week from the opening of classes

3.2. fifty percent (50%): within the period from the second to the fourth week

3.3. No refund: after the fourth week from the opening of classes

3.4. Official Day of Registration. If the withdrawal is made before the opening of classes or made by students who register after the opening of classes and withdraw after that, the number of days shall be counted during the actual date of registration.

3.5. Other Fees. Laboratory fees will not be refunded after one week from the opening of classes where voluntary change is made from one course to another. No refund for registration, library, and miscellaneous fees will be made to any student who withdraws anytime after registration.

Sec. 4. Cross Registration. It is defined as the simultaneous registration for a semester or midyear class in more than one college within the university. The course description should be similar to that of the other college’s subject offering. The permission from the mother college to cross-register should be secured.

4.1. Within the LSPU System. No student shall be registered in any college or campus within the university system without the permission of the campus’ registrar in which the student is primarily enrolled. Request permission to cross-register in another college/ campus should first complete his registration on the campus where he is enrolled. The total number of units or credits for which a student may register in two or more colleges in LSPU should not exceed the maximum number allowed in the academic load rules. Cross-registration may be allowed if there is a conflict in schedule and/or non-offering of the subject in the university.

4.2. From Another Institution. A student registered from another institution and wishes to cross-register in LSPU must present a registrar's written permit. The written permit should state the total number of units for which the student is registered and the subjects that he is authorized to take them at LSPU. Admission is subject to the approval of the LSPU registrar.

4.3. To Another Institution. A student who wishes to cross-register in another institution should seek permission /approval from the college dean where he is enrolled, and the campus registrar on the subject/course and number of units to be cross enrolled to earn credits on the subject/course.

Article 4. Validation

Sec. 1. Validation of Subjects. Subjects are validated without fee when the students will take the examination during the final examination provided such subjects are included in the schedule of examination for regular students. However, validating test may be given during the period covering one week before the first day of registration in each semester upon payment of P20 per unit. The examination is taken at the time is scheduled. Letter request from the Dean must be obtained taking the validation test. Present the approved letter request to the person incharge of validating examination.

Sec. 2. Validation Requirements. The following rules govern the validation requirements:

2.1. holder of a degree who transfers or registers in this University shall be given credits for equivalent courses taken without need for validating them, but such credits shall not exceed 50 percent of the total number of credits required for graduation

2.2. a student transferring from any recognized institution and who possess an Associate course or its equivalent of 72 units of work may be enrolled without validation of subjects sought for advanced credits

2.3. a student transferring from another College/University within LSPU System who has earned units in a program leading to the course to be pursued within the University will be given credits for equivalent courses without validating them

2.4. a student transferring from another College/Campus who has earned units in a degree program other than those pursued in this University will be given credits without validation for selected courses only which in the opinion of the Accreditation Committee will not prejudice the requirements for all courses.

Example: English, Foreign Language, Filipino, Taxation and Land Reform, Population Education (Family Planning), Life and Works of Rizal, New Constitution, Algebra, Plane Trigonometry, Physical Education, NSTP and other similar subjects may not be validated.

However, such subjects as Botany, Zoology, Microbiology, Chemistry, and selected Biological, Physical, and Social Sciences, Engineering Subjects, Accountancy, and all major subjects need validation.

2.5. with the recommendation of the Accreditation Committee, the Dean/Registrar may prescribe or select institutions wherein equivalent courses taken there may not be validated in this University.

Sec. 3. Other Validation Cases

3.1. All other matters concerning validation, herein not fully specified, will be treated on a case-to-case basis.

3.2. Any or all of the above may be set aside in exceptional cases upon the recommendation of the Accreditation Committee/Admission and upon the approval of the University President.

3.3. Application for advanced credit should be make on the prescribed form available and submitted to the Dean of the College and Office of the Registrar.

Article 5. Retention Policies

Sec. 1. Good Moral. After admission to any university program, the student must maintain good moral character at all times regardless of the year level.

Sec. 2. Compliance. A student must adhere to the rules of conduct, regulations, and policies imposed by the University in this Handbook.

Sec. 3. Retention Requirement. A student shall always act in a manner that will uphold the honor, dignity, and prestige of the university and must meet the following requirements to stay in the program and must obtain the corresponding degree:

3.1. Evaluation of student records for purposes of retention is guided by standards.

3.2. Minimum Grade Point Average (GPA) for every semester or summer of study is 2.00.

3.2.1. Minimum Final Grade for Board Program

3.2.1.1. in any major-related subjects 2.00

3.2.1.2. in any professional -related subjects 2.00

3.2.1.3. in any general education subjects 1.75-1.00

3.2.2. Minimum Final Grade for any Non-board Program

3.2.2.1. in any major-required subjects 2.25

3.2.2.2. in any professional-related subjects 2. 25

3.2.2.3. in any general education subjects 2.00-1.00

3.3. In some cases, when a student gets into any of the situations below, the corresponding action shall be applied:

Situation / Action Table:

Situation: 3.1.1. GPA is less than 2.25 (in any board Program)
Action: The student shall be given a probationary status and shall be given intervention programs to improve his/her academic performance.

Situation: 3.1.2. Final Grade in any board/major-related subjects is less than the required grade of 2.25 but not 5.0 (F).
Action: The student shall be given a chance to retake the said subject and meet the required final grade. However, the student shall only be allowed to retake the subject once. The student shall be allowed only a maximum of two major subjects to be retaken from the third year to the study's fourth year.

Situation: 3.1.3. Final Grade in any course-related/professional subjects is less than the required grade of 2.25 but not 5.0 (F).
Action: The student shall be given a chance to retake the said subject and obtain the required final grade. However, the student is only allowed to retake the subject once. The student is allowed only a maximum of two subjects (either course-related subject and/or English subjects) to be retaken during the entire study period.

Situation: 3.1.4. Final Grade in any General Education/Electives Subjects enrolled is 5.00 (F).
Action: The student shall be disqualified from continuing in the chosen Curriculum and shall be advised to shift to another course.

Sec. 4. Scholastic Delinquency The University Academic Council shall promulgate (delinquency) rules and guidelines governing scholastic delinquency, subject to the approval of the Board of Regents, and to the following minimum standards:

4.1. Warning. At the end of the semester, A student who fails 25% to 49% of the total academic units he registered shall be warned by the Dean and advised to improve his/her performance.

4.2. Probation. At the end of the semester, A student fails 50% to 74% of the total academic units in which he registered shall be placed on probation for the succeeding semester, and the Dean concerned shall reduce his/her load accordingly. A student's probation status may be lifted upon passing all the subjects carried during the term s/he is in probation period.

4.3. Dismissal. Any student who fails in more than seventy-five percent (75%) of the total academic units enrolled shall be subject for dismissal from the College; provided that an incomplete grade shall not be included in the computation of earned units; provided further, that this shall not apply to students who receive final grades in only nine academic units.

Sec. 5. Dropped. Any student dropped from one college/school shall not be admitted to another course in LSPU, unless in the evaluation of the Dean, the student’s aptitude and interest may qualify him/her to another field of study, in which case, he/she may be admitted on probation.

Sec. 7. Disqualification Exemption. Permanent disqualification does not apply to cases, where on the recommendation of the Faculty concerned, s/he certifies that the grade of 5.0 is due to the unauthorized dropping off the student and not to poor academic performance.

Percentage of Failure / Allowable Load Table:

Percentage of failure: 25% - 49%
Number of units enrolled: any number of academic units
Status: Warning
Allowable load for the following semester: Less three units from the normal load Percentage of failure: 50% - 74%
Number of units enrolled: 6 academic units or more
Status: Probation
Allowable load for the following semester: 15 units only Percentage of failure: 75% or more
Number of units enrolled: Nine academic units or more
Status: Dismissal from the College
Allowable load for the following semester: not allowed to enroll Percentage of failure: 100%
Number of units enrolled: (none)
Status: Permanent disqualification from the University
Allowable load for the following semester: (none)

Sec. 8. Monitoring and Assessing of the Students’ Status

8.1. Monitoring. To assure that the enrolment or status of the student is correct and aligned, the student who is primarily held responsible for his/her status of enrolment may make use of the following records:

8.1.1. Copy of Grades (COG). The copy of the grade is issued at the end of the semester by the Office of the Registrar to the student where the grades for the course/subjects taken are indicated.

8.1.1.1. Any concern on the student's standing is indicated in the COG, such as INC for completion and/or enrollment status such as warning, probation, dismissal, or disqualification.

8.1.1.2. COG is used by the Dean of the College for the evaluation of the student during enrolment for updating of records.

8.1.2. LSPU Portal. The student may also access his/her academic records or standing by logging into his/her account in the LSPU Portal (myaccount.lspu.edu.ph). Information in the COG is likewise indicated in the account subject for a data privacy policy.

8.1.3. Registrar Visitation. In case any of the options mentioned above are not available, the student may visit the Office of the Registrar to directly consult and/or review his/her enrollment status to check deficiencies and related concerns.

8.2. Consultation on the Status of Enrolment. For any concern on enrollment status, the student shall immediately seek consultation with the Dean of the College for clarification. For further assistance, the student shall be referred to the Office of Registrar. Failure or delayed reporting of the concern, such as changing grades or completing the requirement, may affect the student’s status.

8.3. Candidates for Graduation. For students anticipating graduation, the Office of the Registrar issues a List of Candidates for Graduation for evaluation in case of deficiencies in grades and other university requirements at the start of the first semester of the academic year when the students would graduate.

Article 6. Academic Load and Changes

Sec. 1. Academic Load The student's academic load shall consist of a combination of units of credit, the total of which shall be determined by the student’s curricular program.
